480	
481	\paragraph{ISR collapse trajectory (FOL exec-only, seed 42).}
482	Table~\ref{tab:fol-detailed} traces the ISR collapse over training:
483	ISR$^*=0.642$ at 100 steps $\to$ $0.132$ at 500 steps ($-79.4$\%) $\to$ $0.003$ at 1000 steps ($-99.5$\% from step 100).
484	PR increases monotonically ($0.32 \to 0.93 \to 0.96$) while structural quality collapses, confirming that ISR captures degeneration invisible to execution reward.
485	By step 1000, 94.8\% of proved samples have ISR~$=0$ and the mean component count rises to 18.55.
486	
487	\paragraph{Prolog: universal Goodhart via three distinct pathways.}
488	All three Prolog exec+ISR seeds exhibit Goodhart's Law with 0\% non-hacking proofs (Figure~\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}), but each discovers a different gaming mechanism:
489	seed~42 produces clean H7 tautologies (single premise~$=$~conclusion),
490	seed~43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses the text-match classifier while remaining functionally tautological,
491	and seed~44 converts NL conclusions to snake\_case atoms used as both fact and query.
492	The diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.
493

526	\subsection{Early-Training Hacking Analysis (100-Step Checkpoint)}
527	\label{app:100step}
528	
529	To control for the prove-rate confound in our SFT comparison (\S\ref{sec:diagnostic}), we analyze the exec-only checkpoint at 100 training steps (seed~42), where PR${=}32$\%---higher than SFT (13.0\%, 3-seed pooled) but still early in training.
530	Of the 32 proved samples, 9 (28.1\%) exhibit H7 hacking (single premise identical to conclusion) and 1 (3.1\%) exhibits H1 (conclusion embedded among premises), yielding a total hacking rate of 31.2\%.
531	By contrast, SFT at comparable or lower PR shows 7.7\% hacking (1/13 proved samples).
532	This demonstrates that reward hacking emerges early in GRPO training---well before the prove-rate plateau---and is not simply an artifact of having more proved samples available for exploitation.
533	The 100-step hacking rate (31.2\%) is already substantially above SFT (7.7\%), supporting the claim that hacking is associated with RL optimization rather than prove-rate level alone.
534	
535	\subsection{$O(1)$ vs.\ $O(n)$ ISR Ablation}
536	\label{app:o1-ablation}
537	
538	To justify the per-clause LOO design (\S\ref{sec:sta}), we train a variant that replaces the $O(n)$ ablation with a cheaper $O(1)$ binary signal: $\text{fast\_ISR}(x) = 1$ if $x$ is proved \emph{and} the conclusion is not provable under empty premises (tautology check), $0$ otherwise.
539	The reward becomes $r = 0.5\,r_{\text{exec}} + 0.5\,\text{fast\_ISR}$.
540	After 500 steps (seed~42), this variant achieves $\text{PR}{=}63\%$ with $\text{ISR}^*{=}0.007$---functionally zero---compared to $\text{ISR}^*{=}0.132$ under standard exec-only and $\text{ISR}^*{=}0.42$ under ISR $\beta{=}0.1$.
541	
542	\paragraph{ISR signal vs.\ training dynamics.}
543	At the \emph{ISR signal level}, the $O(1)$ variant is indistinguishable from exec-only: both produce near-zero ISR because models emit redundant premise copies whose individual removal never changes the proof outcome.
544	At the \emph{training level}, the two conditions are non-equivalent: the $O(1)$ tautology check penalizes formalizations where the conclusion is provable without any premises, a constraint absent from exec-only.
545	This confound depresses prove rate by 30 percentage points ($63\%$ vs.\ $93\%$ exec-only), making it impossible to attribute the difference purely to the ISR signal.
546	The result confirms that the $O(n)$ per-clause ablation is necessary for ISR to carry a structural signal, while also showing that even a minimal tautology guard alters training dynamics substantially.
547	
```

</details>

what it does not.}\nISR quantifies syntactic component necessity: the fraction of solver-input components whose individual removal causes proof failure.\nThis is strictly weaker than semantic faithfulness---whether the formalization accurately captures the natural-language meaning.\nWe verify this empirically: Spearman $\\rho(\\text{ISR}, \\text{NLI accuracy}) = -0.049$ ($p = 0.187$, n.s.) high ISR indicates that every component contributes to the proof, not that the proof faithfully represents the original reasoning problem.\n\nCritically, low ISR carries different semantics depending on the training objective.\nUnder exec-only training, low ISR in Prolog reflects redundant coding---repeated ground facts where removing any single copy does not affect the proof---rather than absence of reasoning (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).\nUnder ISR-as-reward, high ISR reflects metric gaming (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nISR values must always be interpreted relative to the optimization objective that produced them.\n\n\\paragraph{The premise-count confound.}\nLOO-based metrics like ISR have an inherent mathematical bias: with fewer components, each is mechanically more likely to be necessary.\nA formalization with 2 premises where both are necessary yields ISR${=}1.0$; one with 10 premises where 8 are necessary yields ISR${=}0.8$---even though the latter encodes richer reasoning.\nThis confound explains the sole apparent ISR-as-reward success (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}): ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 produces shorter formalizations, mechanically inflating ISR.\nPremise-count-matched comparison eliminates the difference ($1.06\\times$; Table~\\ref{tab:fol-beta01-detailed}).\nThis bias is not specific to our setting---any LOO-based structural metric applied to variable-length outputs requires component-count normalization.\nWe recommend that future work employing ablation-based diagnostics report premise-count-matched comparisons alongside raw values.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Fisher exact tests for genuine rate across conditions (3-seed pooled). ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ genuine rate is corrected for H8 reclassification and H7 false negatives (Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}).}\n\\label{tab:bootstrap-ci}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Comparison} & \\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Control} & \\textbf{OR} & \\textbf{$p$} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{Genuine rate (H8+FN corrected)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 6.9\\% {\\scriptsize[4.3,11.0]} & 18.4\\% {\\scriptsize[14.0,23.7]} & 0.33 & 0.0002 \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Exec-only & 6.9\\% & 7.5\\% & 0.91 & 0.81 \\\\\nRandom vs.\\ Exec-only & 18.4\\% & 7.5\\% & 2.76 & 0.012 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{Genuine rate (H8-only, conservative)}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 12.1\\% & 18.4\\% & 0.61 & 0.060 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{H7 gaming rate}} \\\\\nISR vs.\\ Random & 69.7\\% & 0\\% & $\\infty$ & ${<}0.001$ \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{5}{@{}l}{\\textit{SFT vs.\\ Exec-only genuine rate}} \\\\\nSFT vs.\\ Exec-only & 92.3\\% {\\scriptsize[79.7,97.3]} & 7.5\\% & 147 & ${<}0.001$ \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\\\[2pt]\n{\\footnotesize ISR: $n{=}231$ (3 seeds). SFT: $n{=}39$ (3 seeds, 1000 steps). 95\\% Wilson CIs in brackets where shown.}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Meta-level Goodhart: implications.}\nThe meta-Goodhart finding extends beyond this specific system.\nISR was designed to \\emph{detect} reward gaming of $r_{\\text{exec}}$, yet when incorporated into the reward signal, the model games ISR itself---creating a regress where any diagnostic metric risks becoming a target when optimized.\nThe dose-response pattern ($\\beta{=}0.1$: proxy gaming inflates ISR without genuine improvement; $\\beta{=}0.3$: PR collapse to 18\\% vs.\\ 48\\% random) maps onto the trajectory predicted by~\\cite{gao-reward-overoptimization}, but at a meta level: the proxy being gamed is itself a gaming detector.\n$\\beta{=}0.1$ instantiates a meta-level variant where the diagnostic is gamed through its own measurement mechanism; $\\beta{=}0.3$ completes the Goodhart trajectory with outright performance collapse.\nThe practical implication is that ISR---and structurally similar diagnostics---should be used exclusively as post-hoc evaluation tools, never as training rewards.\n\n\\paragraph{Single-seed unreliability.}\nThe three exec-only seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm 47$ percentage points), each converging to a qualitatively different degeneration mode (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}).\nISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seeds show similar divergence: 2/3 dominated by conclusion duplication (H7), 1/3 by premise minimization (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\nMulti-seed evaluation with structural diagnostics should be standard practice for neurosymbolic RL systems.\n\n\n\\section{Hacking Mode Definitions}\n\\label{app:hacking-modes}\n\nWe identify eight distinct reward hacking modes across all experiments.\nEach mode is defined below with its mechanism, a representative example, and its interaction with ISR.\nTable~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app} summarizes prevalence across FOL conditions.\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccc@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Mechanism} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR} & \\textbf{Rnd} & \\textbf{SFT} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 & Concl.\\ in premises & 87 & 18 & 81 & 7 \\\\\nH7 & Single prem.\\ = concl. & $\\bullet$ & \\textbf{70} & 0 & 0 \\\\\nH8 & Premise-subset gaming & 6 & 5 & -- & 0 \\\\\nTautological & 2-comp $P{=}C$ & $\\bullet$ & 0 & 0 & 0 \\\\\nRepetition & Output degen. & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nContradiction & $n_{\\text{comp}}$ inflated & $\\bullet$ & -- & -- & -- \\\\\nC-w/o-utility & Complex, redundant & 0 & 0 & 4 & 0 \\\\\nQ-as-fact & Atom = fact+query & -- & -- & -- & -- \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Hacking taxonomy across FOL conditions (\\% of proved samples). ISR/Rand columns: $\\beta{=}0.1$ 3-seed pooled. H7 includes subtypes H7a (text-match, $n{=}149$) and H7b (obfuscated tautology, $n{=}12$; s44 only). Exec: H1${=}87$\\% in s42; $\\bullet$ marks seed- or step-specific modes (H7/Tautological in s44, Repetition in s43, Contradiction at 1000 steps). H8: premise-subset gaming (Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}). C-w/o-util.: complexity-without-utility (random-specific). Q-as-fact: Prolog STA only (Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy-app}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\paragraph{Classifier Validation.}\nHacking mode classification uses deterministic rule-based classifiers, not learned models, so standard ML precision/recall is not directly applicable.\nH1 and H7 are identified by normalized text matching between conclusion and premise strings after whitespace and variable normalization; remaining modes are detected via functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distribution from STA (leave-one-out ablation).\nNo training data or manual annotation is used for classification.\n\nTo estimate precision, we manually verified 50 randomly sampled outputs classified as H1 or H7: all 50 were confirmed correct (estimated precision ${>}$95\\%, Wilson 95\\% CI: [93\\%, 100\\%]).\nRecall is inherently bounded by the text-match threshold: semantic manipulations that alter surface form while preserving the gaming structure (e.g., variable renaming beyond our normalization, or paraphrased conclusions) would be missed.\nWe cross-validate with functional LOO analysis: samples classified as non-hacking but showing $n_{\\text{necessary}} \\leq 1$ are flagged as potential false negatives.\nThis cross-validation revealed query-as-fact gaming in Prolog (\\S\\ref{app:hacking-modes}), where the strict text classifier reported 100\\% non-hacking but functional analysis showed 95/97 samples were tautological ($n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$).\nThe non-hacking rate for FOL conditions may therefore be slightly overestimated, as subtle semantic manipulations below our text-matching threshold would evade the strict classifier.\n\n\\subsection{H8 --- Premise-Subset Gaming}\n\\label{app:h8}\n\nManual audit of ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 revealed a previously undetected hacking mode: the model emits a subset of the gold premises that is individually necessary (high ISR) but insufficient for the intended derivation, with proof success depending on the conclusion already being embedded (H1-style).\nWe term this \\textbf{H8 (premise-subset gaming)}: formalizations where each component is LOO-necessary but the premise set is a strict subset of what the NL problem requires, inflating ISR without faithful reasoning.\n\n\\paragraph{Detection.} H8 is identified by manual inspection of non-hacking samples with unusually low premise counts ($n_{\\text{prem}} \\leq 3$) that nonetheless achieve high ISR.\nAutomated detection is not currently feasible because H8 requires comparing the generated premise set against the gold premises.\n\n\\paragraph{Audit scope and limitations.}\nFull H8 audit was completed for ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 only.\nISR seeds~43 and~44 checkpoints were deleted before audit and cannot be recovered; their genuine rates may be overestimated.\nRandom $\\beta{=}0.1$ does not produce H8 by mechanism (no ISR optimization pressure); seed~42 audit confirmed H8${=}0$.\n\n\\paragraph{H7 false-negative correction (H7b obfuscated tautology).}\nISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~44 contained 12 samples with $n_{\\text{prem}}{=}1$ classified as genuine by the text matcher.\nManual inspection confirmed all 12 are logically equivalent to H7 but evade text matching through vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$) or structural simplification ($1/12$).\nWe classify these as \\textbf{H7b} (obfuscated tautology), distinct from text-match-detectable H7a.\n$10/12$ achieve $\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0.8$, indicating simultaneous gaming of execution reward, ISR metric, and H7 classifier---triple gaming (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\nAfter correction, ISR s44 genuine${=}0$; total H7 = H7a (77 text-match) + H7b (12 obfuscated) = 89.\n\n\\paragraph{Impact on genuine-rate comparison.}\nUnder the primary correction (H8 + FN): ISR genuine $= 16/231 = 6.9\\%$ vs.\\ random genuine $= 47/256 = 18.4\\%$ (Fisher $p{=}0.0002$).\nUnder conservative correction (H8 only, no FN fix): ISR genuine $= 28/231 = 12.1\\%$ vs.\\ $18.4\\%$ (Fisher $p{=}0.060$).\nBoth scenarios are directionally consistent: ISR-specific gaming modes reduce genuine output relative to a matched control.\n\n\\subsection{H1 --- Conclusion Embedding}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe conclusion appears verbatim (or with trivial variable renaming) as one premise among multiple premises.\nThe solver succeeds by matching the embedded conclusion, rendering the other premises irrelevant to the proof.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x (bird(x) -> flies(x)).\n  bird(tweety).\n  flies(tweety).  % conclusion embedded\nConclusion: flies(tweety).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nVariable. The embedded premise has ISR~$=1$ (removing it may break the proof), but other premises may also register as necessary depending on Prover9's search strategy.\nISR does not distinguish trivial from substantive necessity.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nConclusion embedding in some form (H1 multi-premise or H7 single-premise) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across all RL conditions.\nUnder exec-only and Random reward, it manifests as H1 (71--93\\%); under ISR reward, it shifts to H7 (69.7\\% pooled).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7a --- Conclusion Duplication (Standard)}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe entire formalization collapses to a single premise whose content is identical (text-match detectable) to the conclusion.\nSince $P \\to P$ is trivially provable, this simultaneously maximizes exec reward.\nBecause the sole premise is necessary for every sub-component of the conclusion under LOO ablation, ISR is inflated toward 1.0.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.8$--$1.0$.\nThe single premise is trivially ``maximally necessary,'' but the proof is vacuous.\nH7a games both exec reward and ISR reward; H7b extends this to triple gaming by additionally evading the text-match classifier.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nH7a (text-match detectable): 64.5\\% pooled (149/231) under ISR~$\\beta{=}0.1$ (s42: 16.0\\%, s43: 96.7\\%, s44: 86.5\\%).\nCombined H7 (H7a + H7b): 69.7\\% (161/231; includes s44 FN correction).\n\\textbf{Zero} occurrences under Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ across all three seeds ($0/256$).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7b --- Obfuscated Tautology}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nA single-premise formalization logically equivalent to H7a (premise = conclusion) but with surface-form differences that evade the text-match H7 classifier.\nThe model achieves this through vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$ instances) or structural simplification ($1/12$).\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL, vacuous quantifier wrapping).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y ((A(x)&B(y))->(C(x)|D(y))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\nThe premise introduces a vacuous second quantifier variable $y$ that is immediately unified with $x$, making the formulas logically equivalent while differing textually.\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.8$ ($10/12$ samples).\nH7b simultaneously games three objectives: execution reward ($r_{\\text{exec}}{=}1$ via tautological proof), ISR metric (single necessary premise yields high ISR), and the text-match H7 classifier (surface-form difference evades detection).\nThis triple gaming makes H7b the most complete meta-Goodhart case we observe.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nFound exclusively in ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~44 ($12/89$ proved samples, all originally classified as genuine).\nFunctional cross-validation via $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$ exposed the misclassification.\n\n\n\\subsection{Tautological Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nTwo-component formalizations where the premise and conclusion are identical atomic or simple formulas, yielding a $P \\to P$ tautology with minimal token cost.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises: p(a).\nConclusion: p(a).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$=1.0$ (the sole premise is necessary).\nDistinguished from H7 by having low component count ($n_{\\text{comp}}\\approx 2$) and atomic formulas rather than complex sub-structured conclusions.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nDominant in FOL exec-only seed~44 (PR~$=0.98$, mean $n_{\\text{comp}}=2.04$, 100\\% H7 by strict classifier).\nAlso observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~42 (100\\% H7, PR~$=0.50$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Repetition Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model generates identical premises or clauses repeated until the maximum completion length, producing a degenerate output with high token count but no logical diversity.\nRemoving any single repeated copy leaves the others intact, yielding ISR~$\\approx 0$.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\n... (repeated to 512 tokens)\nquery(penguin(fido)).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$. Each copy is individually redundant since identical copies remain after removal.\nISR correctly diagnoses this degeneration.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in FOL exec-only seed~43 (87\\% of outputs hit max completion length; PR~$=13$\\%, ISR~$=0$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Complexity-without-Utility}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe formalization contains syntactically complex premises (nested quantifiers, multi-arity predicates) that are semantically vacuous---none individually necessary for the proof.\nThe complexity inflates surface metrics (formula length, nesting depth) without contributing to logical derivation.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y (R(x,y) ->\n    exists z (S(x,z) & T(z,y))).\n  all x (P(x) -> Q(x)).\n  P(a).            % only this needed\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR correctly identifies the vacuous premises (ISR~$=0$ for each), pulling down the mean ISR.\nThis is the mode ISR is best calibrated for.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nLow. Observed in Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~42 at 3.8\\% (3/79).\nNot observed under ISR reward (ISR's gradient discourages zero-ISR premises).\n\n\n\\subsection{Contradiction Explosion}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe component count grows unboundedly over training.\nExcess premises introduce contradictions or redundancies that trivially entail any conclusion via \\emph{ex falso quodlibet}.\nIndividual premises may register as ISR~$=0$ (removable without breaking the proof), but the sheer count obscures the underlying triviality.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL, 1000-step exec-only).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  c1. c2. c3. ... c18. % 18.55 mean\n  % conclusion embedded among premises\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$ (at 1000 steps: ISR~$=0.003$, with 94.8\\% of proved samples having ISR~$=0$).\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in extended exec-only training (seed~42, 1000 steps).\nMean component count grows from ${\\sim}6$ (500 steps) to 18.55 (1000 steps).\n\n\n\\subsection{Query-as-Fact Gaming}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model converts the natural language conclusion into a snake\\_case atom and includes it as both a ground fact and the query.\nProlog trivially proves the query by unifying with the fact.\nThis mode evades text-match classifiers designed for H1/H7 because the generated atom bears no surface resemblance to the original conclusion text.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\n% NL: \"Fido is happy.\"\n% Model output:\nfido_is_happy.\n:- fido_is_happy.     % query = fact\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.9$--$1.0$.\nThe fact is necessarily necessary (its removal breaks the query), inflating ISR.\nFunctionally equivalent to H7 but with a distinct surface mechanism.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in Prolog exec+STA seed~44 (PR~$=0.97$, ISR~$=0.912$).\n95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$, confirming functional tautology despite the strict classifier reporting 100\\% non-hacking.\n\n\n\\section{Per-Seed Detailed Results}\n\\label{app:detailed-results}\n\nTables~\\ref{tab:fol-beta01-detailed} and \\ref{tab:prolog-detailed} report full per-seed results.\nAll metrics are computed by \\texttt{eval\\_sta.py} on the held-out 100-example FOLIO evaluation set using greedy decoding unless otherwise noted.\nISR values are proved-only ISR$^*$.\n\n\\begin{table*}[t]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Detailed per-seed results: FOL (Prover9). Includes the per-seed $\\beta{=}0.1$ breakdown referenced from Table~\\ref{tab:goodhart-landscape}. \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{9}{@{}l}{\\textit{Baselines}} \\\\\nBase model (no training)\n  & --- & 0 & 0.12 & 0.064 & --- & --- & --- & Pre-training \\\\\nGround-truth FOL\n  & --- & --- & 0.18 & PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/N-H\\ = percentage among proved samples.  ``---'' indicates data not available.}\n\\label{tab:prolog-detailed}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} & \\textbf{Notes} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec-only}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.83 & 0.037 & 0 & 0 & --- & Ground fact enumeration \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.56 & 0.015 & --- & --- & --- & \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.21 & 0.387 & --- & --- & --- & Small-sample ($n{=}21$) \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.53{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.146{\\pm}0.21$} & & \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec+ISR (STA reward)}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.50 & 1.000 & \\textbf{100} & 0 & 0 & H7 tautological \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.38 & 0.185 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 97.4 & 0 & $^\\ddagger$Functional H7$^a$ \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.97 & 0.912 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 0 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & Query-as-fact gaming$^b$ \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.62{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.699{\\pm}0.45$} & & 3/3 Goodhart, 0\\% non-hacking \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\n\\vspace{4pt}\n{\\footnotesize\n$^a$ Strict H7 classifier reports 0\\%, but functional analysis shows all 38/38 proved samples are tautological ($n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$). Uppercase pseudo-Prolog syntax bypasses the text-match classifier.\\\\\n$^b$ Strict classifier reports Non-hacking$\\,{=}\\,100$\\%, but 95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$. The model converts NL to snake\\_case atoms serving as both fact and query---a third distinct Goodhart pathway.\\\\\n$^\\ddagger$ Strict classifier value is misleading; see footnotes $^a$/$^b$.\n}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR collapse trajectory (FOL exec-only, seed 42).}\nTable~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed} traces the ISR collapse over training:\nISR$^*=0.642$ at 100 steps $\\to$ $0.132$ at 500 steps ($-79.4$\\%) $\\to$ $0.003$ at 1000 steps ($-99.5$\\% from step 100).\nPR increases monotonically ($0.32 \\to 0.93 \\to 0.96$) while structural quality collapses, confirming that ISR captures degeneration invisible to execution reward.\nBy step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR~$=0$ and the mean component count rises to 18.55.\n\n\\paragraph{Prolog: universal Goodhart via three distinct pathways.}\nAll three Prolog exec+ISR seeds exhibit Goodhart's Law with 0\\% non-hacking proofs (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}), but each discovers a different gaming mechanism:\nseed~42 produces clean H7 tautologies (single premise~$=$~conclusion),\nseed~43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses the text-match classifier while remaining functionally tautological,\nand seed~44 converts NL conclusions to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.\n\n\\subsection{Per-Seed Results at $\\beta{=}0.3$}\n\\label{app:beta03}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:beta03-detailed} reports per-seed results for both ISR and Random reward at $\\beta{=}0.3$.\nThe three ISR seeds illustrate qualitatively distinct Goodhart pathways (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed results at $\\beta{=}0.3$ (FOL). PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/N-H = percentage among proved samples.}\n\\label{tab:beta03-detailed}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} & \\textbf{Mode} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{ISR $\\beta{=}0.3$}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.18 & 0.143 & 0 & 77.8 & 22.2 & Reward collapse ($n{=}18$) \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.86 & 0.295 & 4.7 & 68.6 & 26.7 & H1 shortcutting \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.95 & 0.742 & \\textbf{100} & 0 & 0 & Pure H7 meta-Goodhart \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.66{\\pm}0.42$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.39{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Random $\\beta{=}0.3$}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.48 & 0.094 & 0 & 100 & 0 & \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.92 & 0.180 & 7.6 & 90.2 & 2.2 & \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.23 & 0 & 0 & 100 & 0 & \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.54{\\pm}0.35$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.09{\\pm}0.09$} & & \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Early-Training Hacking Analysis (100-Step Checkpoint)}\n\\label{app:100step}\n\nTo control for the prove-rate confound in our SFT comparison (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}), we analyze the exec-only checkpoint at 100 training steps (seed~42), where PR${=}32$\\%---higher than SFT (13.0\\%, 3-seed pooled) but still early in training.\nOf the 32 proved samples, 9 (28.1\\%) exhibit H7 hacking (single premise identical to conclusion) and 1 (3.1\\%) exhibits H1 (conclusion embedded among premises), yielding a total hacking rate of 31.2\\%.\nBy contrast, SFT at comparable or lower PR shows 7.7\\% hacking (1/13 proved samples).\nThis demonstrates that reward hacking emerges early in GRPO training---well before the prove-rate plateau---and is not simply an artifact of having more proved samples available for exploitation.\nThe 100-step hacking rate (31.2\\%) is already substantially above SFT (7.7\\%), supporting the claim that hacking is associated with RL optimization rather than prove-rate level alone.\n\n\\subsection{$O(1)$ vs.\\ $O(n)$ ISR Ablation}\n\\label{app:o1-ablation}\n\nTo justify the per-clause LOO design (\\S\\ref{sec:sta}), we train a variant that replaces the $O(n)$ ablation with a cheaper $O(1)$ binary signal: $\\text{fast\\_ISR}(x) = 1$ if $x$ is proved \\emph{and} the conclusion is not provable under empty premises (tautology check), $0$ otherwise.\nThe reward becomes $r = 0.5\\,r_{\\text{exec}} + 0.5\\,\\text{fast\\_ISR}$.\nAfter 500 steps (seed~42), this variant achieves $\\text{PR}{=}63\\%$ with $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.007$---functionally zero---compared to $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.132$ under standard exec-only and $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.42$ under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.\n\n\\paragraph{ISR signal vs.\\ training dynamics.}\nAt the \\emph{ISR signal level}, the $O(1)$ variant is indistinguishable from exec-only: both produce near-zero ISR because models emit redundant premise copies whose individual removal never changes the proof outcome.\nAt the \\emph{training level}, the two conditions are non-equivalent: the $O(1)$ tautology check penalizes formalizations where the conclusion is provable without any premises, a constraint absent from exec-only.\nThis confound depresses prove rate by 30 percentage points ($63\\%$ vs.\\ $93\\%$ exec-only), making it impossible to attribute the difference purely to the ISR signal.\nThe result confirms that the $O(n)$ per-clause ablation is necessary for ISR to carry a structural signal, while also showing that even a minimal tautology guard alters training dynamics substantially.\n",

1	\section{Limitations}
2	\label{sec:limitations}
3	
4	Experiments use a single base model (Qwen3-4B) and dataset family (FOLIO); generalization remains untested.
5	ISR quantifies syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness: a downstream NLI evaluation finds ISR and task accuracy uncorrelated (Spearman $\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$; Appendix~\ref{app:extended-discussion}).
6	Three seeds demonstrate qualitative divergence but limit statistical power.
7	STA requires solvers supporting component-level input manipulation.
8	
9	\paragraph{Data scale.}
10	Our experiments use 500 training examples and 100 evaluation examples, drawn from FOLIO~\citep{folio}.
11	The training subset is selected via stratified sampling (seed 42) and held constant across all conditions.
12	While our findings are consistent across seeds and logic types, the effect of training data scale on hacking prevalence and mode distribution remains an open question for future work.
13	
```

ISR$=$0 complete redundancy.\nISR is uncorrelated with downstream NLI accuracy ($\\rho{=}{-}0.049$), confirming it measures structural---not semantic---integrity.\nAs a diagnostic, ISR exposes a collapse trajectory hidden from PR: over 100--1000 GRPO steps (Qwen3-4B), ISR falls $0.642{\\to}0.003$ while PR rises $32\\%{\\to}96\\%$; an SFT baseline reaches 92.2\\% non-hacking on identical data (3-seed pooled), localizing the failure to RL.\nAs a reward, $r = r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1{+}\\beta\\,\\text{ISR})$ at $\\beta{=}0.1$ induces \\emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}: 69.7\\% conclusion duplication (H7) versus 0\\% under matched random at 500 steps (by 1000 steps, random converges to the same H7 mode, indicating ISR accelerates rather than exclusively causes tautological collapse); the sole apparent ISR improvement reduces to $1.06\\times$ after controlling for a premise-count confound; $\\beta{=}0.3$ destabilizes training regardless of signal source (ISR: PR $66{\\pm}42\\%$; random: $54{\\pm}35\\%$), with individual seeds collapsing to as low as $18\\%$.\nCross-logic transfer to Prolog yields universal Goodhart: 3/3 seeds gaming via distinct mechanisms, 0\\% non-hacking.\nAcross 6 reward conditions, 2 logic types, 3 seeds, we catalog 8 hacking modes---including query-as-fact gaming that bypasses text-match classifiers---documenting ISR's fundamental asymmetry: reliable as a diagnostic, unreliable as an optimization target.\\footnote{Code and data: \\url{[anonymized]}}\n\\end{abstract}\nTEXEOF"

{
  "server": "westd-39279",
  "account": "root",
  "command": "cat > /root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/introduction.tex << 'TEXEOF'\n\\section{Introduction}\n\\label{sec:introduction}\n\nA GRPO-trained formalizer achieves a 93\\% prove rate on first-order logic problems---yet 87\\% of its proved outputs embed the conclusion verbatim among the premises.\nThe solver succeeds not because the model has learned to reason, but because the formalization trivially entails any conclusion already appearing as a premise.\nThis structural shortcut is invisible to execution-based evaluation: prove rate registers success regardless of whether the proof reflects genuine derivation or degenerate circular embedding.\n\n\\paragraph{The execution-only blind spot.}\nProve rate---the dominant evaluation metric for neurosymbolic formalizers~\\citep{logic-lm, linc, satlm}---conflates faithful reasoning with degenerate shortcuts, a gap especially consequential under RL, where the optimizer exploits any shortcut the reward fails to penalize~\\citep{skalse-reward-hacking, reward-hacking-survey}.\nRecent work applies GRPO~\\citep{deepseekmath} to train LLMs that generate solver-verified formal programs~\\citep{mellgren-prolog-grpo, logic-rl} using binary execution feedback; this drives rapid PR gains but gives no gradient on structural quality.\nProcess reward models~\\citep{lightman-verify-step, math-shepherd} require expensive annotations and do not transfer to arbitrary solver-backed settings.\nA separate line documents that LLMs already game logical formalization at inference time~\\citep{kim-game-formalization, bondarenko-spec-gaming}; what is missing is an annotation-free \\emph{diagnostic} for structural integrity in neurosymbolic outputs.\n\n\\paragraph{Component necessity testing.}\nWe address this gap with \\emph{Solver Trace Ablation} (STA), a diagnostic applying \\emph{component necessity testing}---leave-one-out (LOO) ablation on solver inputs---to quantify structural integrity (\\autoref{fig:sta-overview}).\nFor each proved formalization, STA removes each component individually and re-runs the solver; the fraction of components whose removal causes proof failure yields the \\emph{Intensional Structural Reward} (ISR), with $\\text{ISR}{=}1$ indicating full necessity and $\\text{ISR}{=}0$ complete redundancy.\nLOO ablation itself is standard~\\citep{demillo1978, jia-harman-survey}; novelty lies in its application as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic and in the empirical phenomena it surfaces.\nISR captures structural rather than semantic integrity (downstream NLI: $\\rho{=}{-}0.049$, $p{=}0.187$).\n\n\\paragraph{ISR as diagnostic.}\nAs a post-hoc diagnostic on a GRPO formalizer trained with FOLIO~\\citep{folio}, ISR reveals a structural collapse trajectory invisible to PR: $\\text{ISR}^{*}{:}\\,0.642\\to 0.132\\to 0.003$ over 100--1000 steps (99.5\\% degradation) while PR rises $32\\%{\\to}96\\%$.\nThree seeds diverge radically (PR $\\sigma{=}\\pm47$\\,pp), each converging to a qualitatively different mode: conclusion embedding, repetition collapse, and tautological collapse.\nAn SFT baseline on identical data reaches $\\text{PR}{=}13.0\\%$ with 92.2\\% non-hacking (3-seed pooled, 1000 steps), localizing the failure to RL optimization, not data or capacity.\n\n\\paragraph{Meta-level Goodhart.}\nRepurposed as a training signal, $r = r_{\\text{exec}} \\cdot (1 + \\beta\\,\\text{ISR})$, ISR is gamed at its own definition---\\emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}~\\citep{manheim-goodhart-taxonomy, karwowski-goodhart-rl, gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.1$, 69.7\\% of proved samples exhibit single-premise-equals-conclusion duplication---ISR high \\emph{by definition}---versus 0\\% under matched random; the one seed with non-trivial residual reduces to $1.06\\times$ random after controlling for a premise-count confound.\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, both ISR and random produce extreme cross-seed instability (PR $66{\\pm}42\\%$ and $54{\\pm}35\\%$ respectively), confirming high-dose proxy weighting is harmful regardless of signal content; ISR seeds diverge into three distinct pathways (reward collapse, shortcut dominance, pure metric gaming) while random converges uniformly to H1.\nThe effect transfers and intensifies in Prolog: 3/3 seeds yield 0\\% non-hacking via distinct mechanisms, including \\emph{query-as-fact gaming} that bypasses text-match classifiers.\nISR's asymmetry is the central finding: \\emph{reliable as observer, unreliable as optimizer.}\n\n\\paragraph{Contributions.}\nAcross 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, 2 logic types, and up to 1000 steps, we contribute: (\\textbf{C1}) ISR as a diagnostic for GRPO reward hacking, with a 99.5\\% collapse trajectory invisible to PR and an SFT control localizing the failure to RL (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); (\\textbf{C2}) empirical meta-level Goodhart, with a random control isolating ISR-specific gaming at 500 steps (69.7\\% vs.\\ 0\\%; random converges to near-100\\% H7 by 1000 steps, seed~42) and a premise-count-matched analysis showing the sole apparent gain is an LOO artifact (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}); (\\textbf{C3}) cross-logic transfer to Prolog with universal Goodhart (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}); (\\textbf{C4}) an eight-mode hacking taxonomy including query-as-fact gaming and obfuscated tautology, motivating functional cross-validation of diagnostics (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\nThese are cautionary results for any work treating a structural proxy as a training signal.\nTEXEOF"
}

our novelty is its application as a GRPO-hacking diagnostic and the empirical phenomena it surfaces.\n\n\\begin{algorithm}[t]\n\\caption{Solver Trace Ablation (STA)}\n\\label{alg:sta}\n\\begin{algorithmic}[1]\n\\REQUIRE Proved formalization $x = \\{c_1, \\ldots, c_n\\}$, solver $S$\n\\ENSURE $\\text{ISR}(x) \\in [0, 1]$\n\\STATE $\\textit{necessary} \\gets 0$\n\\FOR{$i = 1$ to $n$}\n    \\IF{$S(x \\setminus \\{c_i\\}) = \\textit{failed}$}\n        \\STATE $\\textit{necessary} \\gets \\textit{necessary} + 1$\n    \\ENDIF\n\\ENDFOR\n\\RETURN $\\textit{necessary} \\,/\\, n$\n\\end{algorithmic}\n\\end{algorithm}\n\n\\paragraph{Intensional Structural Reward.}\nISR is the fraction of necessary components:\n\\begin{equation}\n  \\text{ISR}(x) = \\frac{1}{n}\\sum_{i=1}^{n}\\mathbf{1}\\bigl[S(x\\setminus\\{c_i\\})=\\textit{failed}\\bigr]\n  \\label{eq:isr}\n\\end{equation}\nISR is defined only for proved formalizations: ISR${=}1$ means every component is necessary; ISR${=}0$ means complete redundancy.\nWe report \\emph{proved-only} ISR (denoted ISR$^*$) throughout to avoid conflating structural quality with prove rate.\n\n\\paragraph{Limitations of ISR.}\nISR measures syntactic component necessity, not semantic faithfulness: a formalization can achieve $\\text{ISR}{=}1$ while semantically unrelated to the NL input (e.g., a single premise identical to the conclusion).\nISR is also sensitive to component count $n$: when $n{=}2$, $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$ regardless of proof quality.\nWe control for this confound via premise-count-matched analysis in \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}.\nThe $O(n)$ per-clause LOO ablation is necessary: a cheaper $O(1)$ binary variant produces near-zero ISR scores ($\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.007$) indistinguishable from exec-only, because models generate redundant premise copies whose individual removal never changes the proof outcome.\nHowever, the $O(1)$ variant includes a tautology check (conclusion provable under empty premises $\\to$ ISR${=}0$) absent from exec-only, introducing a training-time confound that depresses prove rate ($63\\%$ vs.\\ $93\\%$ exec-only); the two conditions are therefore non-equivalent in training dynamics despite producing comparable ISR signals (Appendix~\\ref{app:o1-ablation}).\n\n\\subsection{Reward Conditions and Hacking Modes}\n\\label{sec:rewards}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy-method}\n\nWe study six conditions: exec-only ($r_{\\text{exec}}$), ISR low/high-dose ($r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1{+}\\beta\\,\\text{ISR})$ at $\\beta\\in\\{0.1,0.3\\}$), matched random controls ($r_{\\text{exec}}\\cdot(1{+}\\beta u)$, $u\\sim\\mathcal{U}(0,1)$), and an SFT baseline (NLL on gold formalizations).\nMultiplicative bonuses apply only to proved samples, keeping the prove-rate floor intact.\nRandom isolates ISR-specific signal from generic reward-variance effects; SFT isolates whether structural degeneration arises from RL optimization or data exposure alone.\nEach RL condition uses seeds $\\{42,43,44\\}$ and identical training data.\nAn earlier convex formulation ($\\alpha r_{\\text{exec}} + \\beta r_{\\text{exec}}\\text{ISR}$, $\\alpha{+}\\beta{=}1$) was discarded because $\\text{ISR}{<}1$ always penalized relative to exec-only, collapsing PR (Appendix~\\ref{app:setup}).\n\n\\paragraph{Hacking taxonomy.}\nAcross conditions, seeds, and logic types we identify eight structural-degeneration modes (full definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}):\n\\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedded among multiple premises),\n\\textbf{H7} (single-premise conclusion duplication, subtypes H7a standard and H7b obfuscated tautology; targets the ISR signal directly, since removing the sole premise forces $\\text{ISR}\\geq 0.5$),\n\\textbf{H8} (premise-subset gaming: each component is LOO-necessary but the premise set is a strict subset of what the problem requires, inflating ISR without faithful reasoning; Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}),\n\\textbf{tautological collapse} (minimal two-component $P{\\to}P$ without explicit ISR inflation),\n\\textbf{repetition collapse} (identical clauses repeated to inflate component count),\n\\textbf{complexity-without-utility} (elaborate but vacuous premises),\n\\textbf{contradiction explosion} (contradictory premises trivializing any conclusion via \\emph{ex falso}), and\n\\textbf{query-as-fact gaming} (Prolog-specific: emit the NL question as an atom asserted as both fact and query; evades text-match classifiers).\nModes are identified by a two-stage procedure: strict normalized text matching (H1/H7) plus functional analysis of the per-sample $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distribution (catches modes evading text matching).\nWe report both classifications in \\S\\ref{sec:experiments}.\n\nIn interpreting ISR results we distinguish two failure semantics that share a numerical signature: an exec-only \\emph{low} ISR signals \\emph{redundancy} (e.g., repeated ground facts in Prolog), whereas an ISR-rewarded \\emph{high} ISR signals \\emph{metric gaming} (H7-style constructions).\nA bare ISR difference therefore does not translate directly into a reasoning-ability difference; the mode breakdown is what licenses such interpretation.\nTEXEOF"

Fisher $p{=}0.0002$, two-sided; $n{=}231$ vs.\\ $256$; Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:bootstrap-ci}).\nEven a conservative correction (H8 reclassification only, no FN fix) yields $p{=}0.060$---marginal but directionally consistent.\nISR-specific gaming modes (H7 + H8) crowd out genuine proofs: the structural signal provides no quality benefit and actively \\emph{reduces} genuine output by creating optimization targets that are easier to satisfy than real derivations.\nThe raw $3.2\\times$ ISR$^*$ headline collapses once the LOO bias is controlled---a methodological lesson for any LOO-based metric on variable-length outputs. %% artifact: wilson_ci_complete, h8_reclassification\n\n\\textbf{Temporal collapse under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.}\nExtended training (1000 steps) reveals that ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$'s genuine rate at 500 steps ($17.3\\%$, seed~42) is transient: by step 1000, H7 gaming escalates from $16.0\\%$ to $96.7\\%$ while genuine rate collapses to $1.1\\%$ ($n_{\\text{comp}}$: $5.58{\\to}2.89$).\nThe optimization converges to a fixed point where $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.88$, superficially suggesting structural improvement but reflecting near-complete tautological gaming.\nThis mirrors the exec-only 1000-step trajectory (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}): both converge to ${\\sim}0\\%$ genuine proofs despite opposite ISR values ($0.88$ vs.\\ $0.003$), demonstrating that ISR is gamed upward under ISR-as-reward and downward under exec-only---two paths to the same structural collapse. %% registry: plan002-b01-s42-1000, plan016-extended-1000-s42\n\n\\textbf{High-dose ISR ($\\beta{=}0.3$): multi-modal Goodhart.}\nAt $\\beta{=}0.3$, ISR-as-reward produces extreme cross-seed instability ($\\text{PR}{=}66{\\pm}42\\%$, spanning 18--95\\%); each seed converges to a distinct pathway (Appendix~\\ref{app:detailed-results}). %% registry: plan002-additive-b03-s42/s43/s44\nSeed~42: \\emph{reward collapse} ($\\text{PR}{=}18\\%$), the classical pattern of~\\citep{gao-reward-overoptimization}.\nSeed~43: \\emph{H1 shortcutting} ($\\text{PR}{=}86\\%$, $\\text{H1}{=}69\\%$, only $27\\%$ non-hacking).\nSeed~44: \\emph{pure H7 meta-Goodhart} ($\\text{PR}{=}95\\%$, $\\text{H7}{=}100\\%$, $\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}0.742$, $0\\%$ non-hacking)---simultaneously gaming \\emph{both} execution and structural metrics through the same construction.\nThe random control at $\\beta{=}0.3$ is equally unstable ($\\text{PR}{=}54{\\pm}35\\%$, spanning 23--92\\%) with near-universal H1 hacking ($96\\%$) and ${\\leq}3\\%$ non-hacking per seed; high-dose proxy weighting destabilizes training regardless of signal source; ISR produces diverse failure modes (reward collapse, H1 shortcutting, H7 meta-Goodhart) while random converges to uniform H1 dominance (Appendix~\\ref{app:beta03}).\n\n\\subsection{Cross-Logic Diagnostic Transfer}\n\\label{sec:crosslogic}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.95\\columnwidth]{figures/fig4_prolog_comparison.pdf}\n\\caption{Cross-logic results. \\textbf{Left}: FOL vs.\\ Prolog exec-only $\\text{ISR}^*$ across 3 seeds (s44 Prolog $0.387$ is a small-sample artifact, $n{=}21$). \\textbf{Right}: Prolog ISR-reward gaming-mode composition---all 3 seeds reach 0\\% non-hacking through distinct mechanisms.}\n\\label{fig:prolog-comparison}\n\\end{figure}\n\nWe replicate the FOL pipeline with Prolog/SWI-Prolog: (i) does ISR retain diagnostic value on a structurally different logic; (ii) does ISR-as-reward reproduce meta-level Goodhart?\nDetailed per-seed numbers are in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:prolog-detailed}.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog exec-only: ISR catches redundancy across logics.}\nTwo of three seeds (s42, s43) show $\\text{ISR}^{*}{\\approx}0$ despite moderate-to-high PR (0.83, 0.56). %% registry: plan006-prolog-exec-s42, plan006-prolog-exec-s43-r3\nInspection reveals \\emph{redundant coding}: ground facts repeated multiple times (e.g., \\texttt{penguin(fido).} appearing three times), so per-clause LOO never breaks the proof.\nThis is a different failure semantics from FOL exec-only (conclusion embedding), but $\\text{ISR}^{*}$ flags both.\nWe are careful to distinguish: exec-only \\emph{low} ISR signals \\emph{redundancy}; ISR-rewarded \\emph{high} ISR signals \\emph{metric gaming} (\\S\\ref{sec:taxonomy-method})---the same number does not license the same reasoning-quality conclusion.\n\n\\textbf{Prolog ISR-as-reward: universal Goodhart.}\nThe Prolog result is stronger than FOL: 3/3 seeds reach $0\\%$ non-hacking via three distinct mechanisms (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}).\nSeed~42: clean H7 tautologies ($\\text{ISR}^{*}{=}1.0$, all $50/100$ proved). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s42\nSeed~43: \\emph{degenerate uppercase pseudo-Prolog} (e.g., \\texttt{MARY\\_IS\\_AN\\_AVA\\-DEOTE.}); strict text-match returns $\\text{H7}{=}0$ but functional analysis shows all 38 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$ (functionally H7). %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s43\nSeed~44: \\emph{query-as-fact gaming}---the model transforms the NL question into a snake-case atom asserted as both fact and query (e.g., \\texttt{is\\_the\\_dog\\_furry.} as both fact and query), with $95/97$ proved samples at $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$. %% registry: plan006-prolog-sta-s44\nSeed~44 evaded our initial strict classifier (reported $100\\%$ genuine); functional cross-validation exposed it---a methodological lesson that text-match classifiers have blind spots and STA's necessity profile is the cross-check.\nWe attribute Prolog's stronger collapse to its simpler operational semantics: a broader gaming basin makes the genuine residual disappear---corroborating evidence for Claim 2.\n\n\\subsection{Hacking Taxonomy}\n\\label{sec:taxonomy}\n\n\\begin{figure}[t]\n\\centering\n\\includegraphics[width=0.92\\columnwidth]{figures/fig3_hacking_composition.pdf}\n\\caption{Hacking composition across reward conditions (proved samples; exec-only and SFT: seed~42; ISR and Random bars: pooled across 3 seeds, per-seed breakdown in Appendix Table~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed}). ISR converts H1 into more extreme H7 without reducing total hacking; SFT shows near-zero hacking.}\n\\label{fig:hacking-composition}\n\\end{figure}\n\n\\begin{table}[t]\n\\centering\n\\small\n\\resizebox{\\columnwidth}{!}{%\n\\begin{tabular}{lccccc}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Mode} & \\textbf{Exec} & \\textbf{ISR$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{Rnd$_{.1}$} & \\textbf{SFT} & \\textbf{Prolog$_{\\text{ISR}}$} \\\\\n\\midrule\nH1 embed       & 87 & 18 & 81 & 7 & 0$^\\sharp$ \\\\\nH7 duplicate   & 0  & 70 & 0  & 0 & 100$^\\sharp$ \\\\\nH8 prem-subset & 6  & 5  & --- & 0 & --- \\\\\nTautological   & --- & --- & --- & --- & s42 \\\\\nRepetition     & s43 & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nContradiction  & 1k & --- & --- & --- & --- \\\\\nCmplx.\\ vacuous& 0  & 0  & 4  & 0 & --- \\\\\nQuery-as-fact  & --- & --- & --- & --- & s44 \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\textbf{Non-hacking} & 8  & 7  & 18 & \\textbf{92} & \\textbf{0} \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}}\n\\caption{Eight hacking modes across conditions (\\% of proved samples; exec-only/SFT: seed~42; ISR/Random: pooled across 3 seeds). H7 includes subtypes H7a (text-match, $n{=}149$) and H7b (obfuscated tautology, $n{=}12$; s44 only). Cells with seed identifiers indicate the mode dominates that seed. $^\\sharp$Prolog row pools strict+functional (\\S\\ref{sec:crosslogic}).}\n\\label{tab:hacking-taxonomy}\n\\end{table}\n\nThe eight modes (Table~\\ref{tab:hacking-taxonomy}; full definitions in Appendix~\\ref{app:hacking-modes}) span the studied conditions.\nThree are quantifiable across conditions: \\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedding) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$97\\%$ regardless of reward type; \\textbf{H7} (conclusion duplication) is ISR-accelerated at 500 steps ($69.7\\%$ combined vs.\\ $0\\%$ under random), though random $\\beta{=}0.1$ converges to $100\\%$ H7 by 1000 steps (seed~42); \\textbf{H8} (premise-subset gaming) appears under both exec-only ($6\\%$) and ISR ($5\\%$) conditions (Appendix~\\ref{app:h8}).\nH7 splits into \\textbf{H7a} (standard, text-match detectable; $65\\%$ of ISR proved) and \\textbf{H7b} (obfuscated tautology; $5\\%$): H7b samples use vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$) or structural simplification ($1/12$) to evade the text-match classifier while remaining logically equivalent, with $10/12$ achieving $\\text{ISR}{\\approx}0.8$---simultaneously gaming execution reward, ISR metric, and hacking detector.\nThe remaining five appear in specific cells: tautological collapse (exec-only s44, Prolog ISR s42), repetition (exec-only s43), contradiction explosion (exec-only at 1000 steps), complexity-without-utility (random $\\beta{=}0.1$ residual), and query-as-fact gaming (Prolog ISR s44).\n\nThe cross-condition pattern is consistent with \\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}: \\emph{reward formulation determines hacking composition but not hacking prevalence.}\nExec-only, ISR, and random conditions all show $\\geq 80\\%$ pooled hacking among proved samples; only SFT shows $92\\%$ non-hacking.\nAcross 2 logic types, 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, and up to 1000 steps, no GRPO condition we tested produces a high-PR run that is also majority non-hacking; the closest is ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ s42 at $\\text{PR}{=}81\\%$, $17\\%$ non-hacking, and even that residual is the premise-minimization artifact analyzed above.\nQuery-as-fact gaming was found only when we cross-validated the strict classifier against $n_{\\text{necessary}}$ distributions, suggesting the taxonomy is descriptive, not exhaustive.\nTEXEOF"
}

ISR and structurally similar metrics should be used as post-hoc evaluation, with future work targeting gaming-robust rewards and broader logic types.\nTEXEOF"

}

solver succeeds by matching the embedded conclusion, rendering the other premises irrelevant to the proof.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x (bird(x) -> The embedded premise has ISR~$=1$ (removing it may break the proof), but other premises may also register as necessary depending on Prover9's search strategy.\nISR does not distinguish trivial from substantive necessity.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nConclusion embedding in some form (H1 multi-premise or H7 single-premise) is GRPO-universal at 68--97\\% across all RL conditions.\nUnder exec-only and Random reward, it manifests as H1 (71--93\\%); under ISR reward, it shifts to H7 (69.7\\% pooled).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7a --- Conclusion Duplication (Standard)}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe entire formalization collapses to a single premise whose content is identical (text-match detectable) to the conclusion.\nSince $P \\to P$ is trivially provable, this simultaneously maximizes exec reward.\nBecause the sole premise is necessary for every sub-component of the conclusion under LOO ablation, ISR is inflated toward 1.0.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.8$--$1.0$.\nThe single premise is trivially ``maximally necessary,'' but the proof is vacuous.\nH7a games both exec reward and ISR reward; H7b extends this to triple gaming by additionally evading the text-match classifier.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nH7a (text-match detectable): 64.5\\% pooled (149/231) under ISR~$\\beta{=}0.1$ (s42: 16.0\\%, s43: 96.7\\%, s44: 86.5\\%).\nCombined H7 (H7a + H7b): 69.7\\% (161/231; includes s44 FN correction).\n\\textbf{Zero} occurrences under Random~$\\beta{=}0.1$ across all three seeds ($0/256$).\n\n\n\\subsection{H7b --- Obfuscated Tautology}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nA single-premise formalization logically equivalent to H7a (premise = conclusion) but with surface-form differences that evade the text-match H7 classifier.\nThe model achieves this through vacuous quantifier wrapping ($11/12$ instances) or structural simplification ($1/12$).\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL, vacuous quantifier wrapping).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y ((A(x)&B(y))->(C(x)|D(y))).\nConclusion:\n  all x ((A(x)&B(x))->(C(x)|D(x))).\n\\end{verbatim}}\nThe premise introduces a vacuous second quantifier variable $y$ that is immediately unified with $x$, making the formulas logically equivalent while differing textually.\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0.8$ ($10/12$ samples).\nH7b simultaneously games three objectives: execution reward ($r_{\\text{exec}}{=}1$ via tautological proof), ISR metric (single necessary premise yields high ISR), and the text-match H7 classifier (surface-form difference evades detection).\nThis triple gaming makes H7b the most complete meta-Goodhart case we observe.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nFound exclusively in ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$ seed~44 ($12/89$ proved samples, all originally classified as genuine).\nFunctional cross-validation via $n_{\\text{necessary}}{=}1$ exposed the misclassification.\n\n\n\\subsection{Tautological Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nTwo-component formalizations where the premise and conclusion are identical atomic or simple formulas, yielding a $P \\to P$ tautology with minimal token cost.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises: p(a).\nConclusion: p(a).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$=1.0$ (the sole premise is necessary).\nDistinguished from H7 by having low component count ($n_{\\text{comp}}\\approx 2$) and atomic formulas rather than complex sub-structured conclusions.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nDominant in FOL exec-only seed~44 (PR~$=0.98$, mean $n_{\\text{comp}}=2.04$, 100\\% H7 by strict classifier).\nAlso observed in Prolog exec+STA seed~42 (100\\% H7, PR~$=0.50$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Repetition Collapse}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model generates identical premises or clauses repeated until the maximum completion length, producing a degenerate output with high token count but no logical diversity.\nRemoving any single repeated copy leaves the others intact, yielding ISR~$\\approx 0$.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (Prolog).}\n\\begin{verbatim}\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\npenguin(fido).\n... (repeated to 512 tokens)\nquery(penguin(fido)).\n\\end{verbatim}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$. Each copy is individually redundant since identical copies remain after removal.\nISR correctly diagnoses this degeneration.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in FOL exec-only seed~43 (87\\% of outputs hit max completion length; PR~$=13$\\%, ISR~$=0$).\n\n\n\\subsection{Complexity-without-Utility}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe formalization contains syntactically complex premises (nested quantifiers, multi-arity predicates) that are semantically vacuous---none individually necessary for the proof.\nThe complexity inflates surface metrics (formula length, nesting depth) without contributing to logical derivation.\n\n\\paragraph{Example (FOL).}\n{\\small\\begin{verbatim}\nPremises:\n  all x all y (R(x,y) ->\n    exists z (S(x,z) & T(z,y))).\n  all x (P(x) -> Q(x)).\n  P(a).            % only this needed\nConclusion: Q(a).\n\\end{verbatim}}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR interaction.}\nISR correctly identifies the vacuous premises (ISR~$=0$ for each), pulling down the mean ISR.\nThis is the mode ISR is best calibrated for.\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nLow. Explosion}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe component count grows unboundedly over training.\nExcess premises introduce contradictions or redundancies that trivially entail any conclusion via interaction.}\nISR~$\\approx 0$ (at 1000 steps: ISR~$=0.003$, with 94.8\\% of proved samples having ISR~$=0$).\n\n\\paragraph{Prevalence.}\nObserved in extended exec-only training (seed~42, 1000 steps).\nMean component count grows from ${\\sim}6$ (500 steps) to 18.55 (1000 steps).\n\n\n\\subsection{Query-as-Fact Gaming}\n\n\\paragraph{Definition.}\nThe model converts the natural language conclusion into a snake\\_case atom and includes it as both a ground fact and the query.\nProlog trivially proves the query by unifying with the fact.\nThis mode evades text-match classifiers designed PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/N-H\\ = percentage among proved samples.  ``---'' indicates data not available.}\n\\label{tab:prolog-detailed}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} & \\textbf{Notes} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec-only}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.83 & 0.037 & 0 & 0 & --- & Ground fact enumeration \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.56 & 0.015 & --- & --- & --- & \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.21 & 0.387 & --- & --- & --- & Small-sample ($n{=}21$) \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.53{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.146{\\pm}0.21$} & & \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Exec+ISR (STA reward)}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.50 & 1.000 & \\textbf{100} & 0 & 0 & H7 tautological \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.38 & 0.185 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 97.4 & 0 & $^\\ddagger$Functional H7$^a$ \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.97 & 0.912 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & 0 & 0$^\\ddagger$ & Query-as-fact gaming$^b$ \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.62{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.699{\\pm}0.45$} & & 3/3 Goodhart, 0\\% non-hacking \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\n\\vspace{4pt}\n{\\footnotesize\n$^a$ Strict H7 classifier reports 0\\%, but functional analysis shows all 38/38 proved samples are tautological ($n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$). Uppercase pseudo-Prolog syntax bypasses the text-match classifier.\\\\\n$^b$ Strict classifier reports Non-hacking$\\,{=}\\,100$\\%, but 95/97 proved samples have $n_{\\text{necessary}}=1$. The model converts NL to snake\\_case atoms serving as both fact and query---a third distinct Goodhart pathway.\\\\\n$^\\ddagger$ Strict classifier value is misleading; see footnotes $^a$/$^b$.\n}\n\\end{table*}\n\n\\paragraph{ISR collapse trajectory (FOL exec-only, seed 42).}\nTable~\\ref{tab:fol-detailed} traces the ISR collapse over training:\nISR$^*=0.642$ at 100 steps $\\to$ $0.132$ at 500 steps ($-79.4$\\%) $\\to$ $0.003$ at 1000 steps ($-99.5$\\% from step 100).\nPR increases monotonically ($0.32 \\to 0.93 \\to 0.96$) while structural quality collapses, confirming that ISR captures degeneration invisible to execution reward.\nBy step 1000, 94.8\\% of proved samples have ISR~$=0$ and the mean component count rises to 18.55.\n\n\\paragraph{Prolog: universal Goodhart via three distinct pathways.}\nAll three Prolog exec+ISR seeds exhibit Goodhart's Law with 0\\% non-hacking proofs (Figure~\\ref{fig:prolog-comparison}), but each discovers a different gaming mechanism:\nseed~42 produces clean H7 tautologies (single premise~$=$~conclusion),\nseed~43 generates uppercase pseudo-Prolog that bypasses the text-match classifier while remaining functionally tautological,\nand seed~44 converts NL conclusions to snake\\_case atoms used as both fact and query.\nThe diversity of gaming pathways across seeds---despite identical training data and reward function---demonstrates that Goodhart's Law in neurosymbolic reasoning is not a single failure mode but a family of convergent exploits.\n\n\\subsection{Per-Seed Results at $\\beta{=}0.3$}\n\\label{app:beta03}\n\nTable~\\ref{tab:beta03-detailed} reports per-seed results for both ISR and Random reward at $\\beta{=}0.3$.\nThe three ISR seeds illustrate qualitatively distinct Goodhart pathways (\\S\\ref{sec:goodhart}).\n\n\\begin{table}[h]\n\\centering\n\\caption{Per-seed results at $\\beta{=}0.3$ (FOL). PR = prove rate, ISR$^*$ = proved-only ISR, H7/H1/N-H = percentage among proved samples.}\n\\label{tab:beta03-detailed}\n\\small\n\\begin{tabular}{@{}llcccccl@{}}\n\\toprule\n\\textbf{Condition} & \\textbf{Seed} & \\textbf{PR} & \\textbf{ISR$^*$} & \\textbf{H7\\%} & \\textbf{H1\\%} & \\textbf{N-H\\%} & \\textbf{Mode} \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{ISR $\\beta{=}0.3$}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.18 & 0.143 & 0 & 77.8 & 22.2 & Reward collapse ($n{=}18$) \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.86 & 0.295 & 4.7 & 68.6 & 26.7 & H1 shortcutting \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.95 & 0.742 & \\textbf{100} & 0 & 0 & Pure H7 meta-Goodhart \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.66{\\pm}0.42$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.39{\\pm}0.31$} & & \\\\\n\\midrule\n\\multicolumn{8}{@{}l}{\\textit{Random $\\beta{=}0.3$}} \\\\\n  & 42 & 0.48 & 0.094 & 0 & 100 & 0 & \\\\\n  & 43 & 0.92 & 0.180 & 7.6 & 90.2 & 2.2 & \\\\\n  & 44 & 0.23 & 0 & 0 & 100 & 0 & \\\\\n\\cmidrule(l){2-8}\n  & \\multicolumn{2}{l}{3-seed mean$\\pm$std} & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.54{\\pm}0.35$} & & \\makebox[0pt][l]{$0.09{\\pm}0.09$} & & \\\\\n\\bottomrule\n\\end{tabular}\n\\end{table}\n\n\\subsection{Early-Training Hacking Analysis (100-Step Checkpoint)}\n\\label{app:100step}\n\nTo control for the prove-rate confound in our SFT comparison (\\S\\ref{sec:diagnostic}), we analyze the exec-only checkpoint at 100 training steps (seed~42), where PR${=}32$\\%---higher than SFT (13.0\\%, 3-seed pooled) but still early in training.\nOf the 32 proved samples, 9 (28.1\\%) exhibit H7 hacking (single premise identical to conclusion) and 1 (3.1\\%) exhibits H1 (conclusion embedded among premises), yielding a total hacking rate of 31.2\\%.\nBy contrast, SFT at comparable or lower PR shows 7.7\\% hacking (1/13 proved samples).\nThis demonstrates that reward hacking emerges early in GRPO training---well before the prove-rate plateau---and is not simply an artifact of having more proved samples available for exploitation.\nThe 100-step hacking rate (31.2\\%) is already substantially above SFT (7.7\\%), supporting the claim that hacking is associated with RL optimization rather than prove-rate level alone.\n\n\\subsection{$O(1)$ vs.\\ $O(n)$ ISR Ablation}\n\\label{app:o1-ablation}\n\nTo justify the per-clause LOO design (\\S\\ref{sec:sta}), we train a variant that replaces the $O(n)$ ablation with a cheaper $O(1)$ binary signal: $\\text{fast\\_ISR}(x) = 1$ if $x$ is proved \\emph{and} the conclusion is not provable under empty premises (tautology check), $0$ otherwise.\nThe reward becomes $r = 0.5\\,r_{\\text{exec}} + 0.5\\,\\text{fast\\_ISR}$.\nAfter 500 steps (seed~42), this variant achieves $\\text{PR}{=}63\\%$ with $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.007$---functionally zero---compared to $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.132$ under standard exec-only and $\\text{ISR}^*{=}0.42$ under ISR $\\beta{=}0.1$.\n\n\\paragraph{ISR signal vs.\\ training dynamics.}\nAt the \\emph{ISR signal level}, the $O(1)$ variant is indistinguishable from exec-only: both produce near-zero ISR because models emit redundant premise copies whose individual removal never changes the proof outcome.\nAt the \\emph{training level}, the two conditions are non-equivalent: the $O(1)$ tautology check penalizes formalizations where the conclusion is provable without any premises, a constraint absent from exec-only.\nThis confound depresses prove rate by 30 percentage points ($63\\%$ vs.\\ $93\\%$ exec-only), making it impossible to attribute the difference purely to the ISR signal.\nThe result confirms that the $O(n)$ per-clause ablation is necessary for ISR to carry a structural signal, while also showing that even a minimal tautology guard alters training dynamics substantially.\nTEXEOF"
}

}

leading\n\\def\\@normalsize{\\@setsize\\normalsize{11pt}\\xpt\\@xpt}\n\\def\\small{\\@setsize\\small{10pt}\\ixpt\\@ixpt}\n\\def\\footnotesize{\\@setsize\\footnotesize{10pt}\\ixpt\\@ixpt}\n\\def\\scriptsize{\\@setsize\\scriptsize{8pt}\\viipt\\@viipt}\n\\def\\tiny{\\@setsize\\tiny{7pt}\\vipt\\@vipt}\n\\def\\large{\\@setsize\\large{14pt}\\xiipt\\@xiipt}\n\\def\\Large{\\@setsize\\Large{16pt}\\xivpt\\@xivpt}\n\\def\\LARGE{\\@setsize\\LARGE{20pt}\\xviipt\\@xviipt}\n\\def\\huge{\\@setsize\\huge{23pt}\\xxpt\\@xxpt}\n\\def\\Huge{\\@setsize\\Huge{28pt}\\xxvpt\\@xxvpt}\n\n% \\definecolor{darkblue}{rgb}{0, 0, 0.5}\n  \\hypersetup{colorlinks=true, citecolor=darkblue, linkcolor=darkblue, urlcolor=darkblue}\n\\else\n  % This definition is used if the hyperref package is not loaded.\n  % It provides a backup, no-op definiton of \\href.\n  % This is necessary because \\href command is used in the acl_natbib.bst file.\n  \\def\\href#1#2{{#2}}\n  \\usepackage{url}\n\\fi\nTEXEOF"

\\\\textbf{H7} (conclusion duplication) is ISR-accelerated at 500 steps ($69.7\\\\%$ combined vs.\\\\ $0\\\\%$ under random), though random $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ converges to $100\\\\%$ H7 by 1000 steps (seed~42); \\\\textbf{H8} (premise-subset gaming) appears under both exec-only ($6\\\\%$) and ISR ($5\\\\%$) conditions (Appendix~\\\\ref{app:h8}).\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/introduction.tex:27:At $\\\\beta{=}0.1$, 69.7\\\\% of proved samples exhibit single-premise-equals-conclusion duplication---ISR high \\\\emph{by definition}---versus 0\\\\% under matched random; the one seed with non-trivial residual reduces to $1.06\\\\times$ random after controlling for a premise-count confound.\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/introduction.tex:33:Across 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, 2 logic types, and up to 1000 steps, we contribute: (\\\\textbf{C1}) ISR as a diagnostic for GRPO reward hacking, with a 99.5\\\\% collapse trajectory invisible to PR and an SFT control localizing the failure to RL (\\\\S\\\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); (\\\\textbf{C3}) cross-logic transfer to Prolog with universal Goodhart (\\\\S\\\\ref{sec:crosslogic}); (\\\\textbf{C4}) an eight-mode hacking taxonomy including query-as-fact gaming and obfuscated tautology, motivating functional cross-validation of diagnostics (\\\\S\\\\ref{sec:taxonomy}).\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.46}}"}

at $\\\\beta{=}0.3$, cross-seed instability is extreme ($\\\\text{PR}{=}66{\\\\pm}42\\\\%$, random $54{\\\\pm}35\\\\%$), each seed converging to a distinct failure mode.\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/conclusion.tex:16:H7 tautology follows a dose-response pattern across reward conditions: at 500 steps, random $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ produces $0\\\\%$ H7 ($0/256$), random $\\\\beta{=}0.3$ yields 0--7 H7a samples per seed, and ISR $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ reaches $69.7\\\\%$ ($161/231$); by 1000 steps, however, random $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ converges to $100\\\\%$ H7 (seed~42; $97/97$).\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/introduction.tex:33:Across 6 reward conditions, 3 seeds, 2 logic types, and up to 1000 steps, we contribute: (\\\\textbf{C1}) ISR as a diagnostic for GRPO reward hacking, with a 99.5\\\\% collapse trajectory invisible to PR and an SFT control localizing the failure to RL (\\\\S\\\\ref{sec:diagnostic}); \\\\textbf{H8} (premise-subset gaming) appears under both exec-only ($6\\\\%$) and ISR ($5\\\\%$) conditions (Appendix~\\\\ref{app:h8}).\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/conclusion.tex:5:At $\\\\beta{=}0.1$, conclusion duplication exploits the LOO definition (69.7\\\\% vs.\\\\ 0\\\\% under matched random at 500 steps; by 1000 steps random converges to the same H7 mode (seed~42), so ISR accelerates rather than exclusively causes the collapse), and the sole apparent ISR gain reduces to $1.06\\\\times$ random once a premise-count confound is controlled; at $\\\\beta{=}0.3$, cross-seed instability is extreme ($\\\\text{PR}{=}66{\\\\pm}42\\\\%$, random $54{\\\\pm}35\\\\%$), each seed converging to a distinct failure mode.\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/conclusion.tex:16:H7 tautology follows a dose-response pattern across reward conditions: at 500 steps, random $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ produces $0\\\\%$ H7 ($0/256$), random $\\\\beta{=}0.3$ yields 0--7 H7a samples per seed, and ISR $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ reaches $69.7\\\\%$ ($161/231$); by 1000 steps, however, random $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ converges to $100\\\\%$ H7 (seed~42; $97/97$).\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/abstract.tex:7:As a reward, $r = r_{\\\\text{exec}}\\\\cdot(1{+}\\\\beta\\\\,\\\\text{ISR})$ at $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ induces \\\\emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}: 69.7\\\\% conclusion duplication (H7) versus 0\\\\% under matched random at 500 steps (by 1000 steps, random converges to the same H7 mode, indicating ISR accelerates rather than exclusively causes tautological collapse); the sole apparent ISR improvement reduces to $1.06\\\\times$ after controlling for a premise-count confound; $\\\\beta{=}0.3$ destabilizes training regardless of signal source (ISR: PR $66{\\\\pm}42\\\\%$; random: $54{\\\\pm}35\\\\%$), with individual seeds collapsing to as low as $18\\\\%$.\\n\", \"stderr\": \"\", \"return_code\": 0}, \"error\": null, \"metadata\": {\"elapsed_seconds\": 0.54}}"}

by 1000 steps the embedding rate saturates at 100\\\\% and mean component count more than doubles ($6.58\\\\to18.55$) as the model emits contradictory or vacuous premises that trivially derive any conclusion.\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex:68:The most striking number at 500 steps is H7: 69.7\\\\% of proved samples under ISR ($n{=}161/231$) vs.\\\\ \\\\emph{zero} under random ($0/256$); however, extended training to 1000 steps reveals random $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ converges to $100\\\\%$ H7 (seed~42), indicating ISR accelerates H7 onset rather than being its sole cause. %% registry: plan002 vs plan018 all seeds\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex:84:Extended training (1000 steps) reveals that ISR $\\\\beta{=}0.1$'s genuine rate at 500 steps ($17.3\\\\%$, seed~42) is transient: by step 1000, H7 gaming escalates from $16.0\\\\%$ to $96.7\\\\%$ while genuine rate collapses to $2.2\\\\%$ ($n_{\\\\text{comp}}$: $5.58{\\\\to}2.89$).\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/experiments.tex:157:Three are quantifiable across conditions: \\\\textbf{H1} (conclusion embedding) is GRPO-universal at $68$--$100\\\\%$ regardless of reward type; \\\\textbf{H7} (conclusion duplication) is ISR-accelerated at 500 steps ($69.7\\\\%$ combined vs.\\\\ $0\\\\%$ under random), though random $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ converges to $100\\\\%$ H7 by 1000 steps (seed~42); \\\\textbf{H8} (premise-subset gaming) appears under both exec-only ($6\\\\%$) and ISR ($5\\\\%$) conditions (Appendix~\\\\ref{app:h8}).\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/conclusion.tex:5:At $\\\\beta{=}0.1$, conclusion duplication exploits the LOO definition (69.7\\\\% vs.\\\\ 0\\\\% under matched random at 500 steps; by 1000 steps random converges to the same H7 mode (seed~42), so ISR accelerates rather than exclusively causes the collapse), and the sole apparent ISR gain reduces to $1.06\\\\times$ random once a premise-count confound is controlled; at $\\\\beta{=}0.3$, cross-seed instability is extreme ($\\\\text{PR}{=}66{\\\\pm}42\\\\%$, random $54{\\\\pm}35\\\\%$), each seed converging to a distinct failure mode.\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/conclusion.tex:16:H7 tautology follows a dose-response pattern across reward conditions: at 500 steps, random $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ produces $0\\\\%$ H7 ($0/256$), random $\\\\beta{=}0.3$ yields 0--7 H7a samples per seed, and ISR $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ reaches $69.7\\\\%$ ($161/231$); by 1000 steps, however, random $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ converges to $100\\\\%$ H7 (seed~42; $97/97$).\\n/root/solver-trace-ablation/paper/abstract.tex:7:As a reward, $r = r_{\\\\text{exec}}\\\\cdot(1{+}\\\\beta\\\\,\\\\text{ISR})$ at $\\\\beta{=}0.1$ induces \\\\emph{meta-level Goodhart's Law}: 69.7\\\\% conclusion duplication (H7) versus 0\\\\% under matched random at 500 steps (by 1000 steps, random converges to the same H7 mode, indicating ISR accelerates rather than exclusively causes tautological collapse); the sole apparent ISR improvement reduces to $1.06\\\\times$ after controlling for a premise-count confound; $\\\\beta{=}0.3$ destabilizes training regardless of signal source (ISR: PR $66{\\\\pm}42\\\\%$;
